# P2 — Editors release (journals · methods · what changed · match my abstract) — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Ship the editors' release of TCI Research Atlas v2: a journals module built from the corpus (optionally named by `registry/journals.csv`) with metadata completeness (D2), in-house / institutional diversity / foreign authors (D3), peer journals (D4), scope drift (D1) and a special-issue finder (D5); a research-methods lexicon with sample sizes (E11, D7; LLM notes opt-in, topic level only); the quarter diff "what changed" (E5); and "match my abstract" + "where to publish" (R3, R4) computed in the browser — as contract 2.1 files and five new pages `/journals`, `/journals/:id`, `/methods`, `/changes`, `/match`.

**Architecture:** Same batch → checkpoint → JSON design. Three new pipeline steps run after `layout`: `methods` (12_*), `journals` (13_*), `match` (14_*); pure analytics live in small modules (`registry`, `countries`, `journal_metrics`, `journal_topics`, `methods`, `matcher`, `diff`) and `export_editors.py` turns the checkpoints into contract 2.1 models that `export_v2.build_release` adds to the release (each module optional, listed in `manifest.modules`). The quarter diff compares the release with the previous release's compact snapshot kept in `state_dir/releases/<release>/snapshot.json` (fallback: the previous release folder). The web loads the new files lazily (`useReleaseFile`), the five pages are lazy routes, and the matcher re-implements the pipeline's analyzer (fold + char trigrams, pinned by a shared parity fixture) so no text leaves the browser.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12 via `uv` — pandas 3, numpy, scipy (Jensen–Shannon), scikit-learn 1.9 (`CountVectorizer`, `normalize`), pydantic v2, typer, pytest, ruff (no new dependency). Web — the P1b/P1c pins unchanged: React 19.3.1, react-router 7.18.4 (`lazy` + `Suspense`), Vite 7.3.7 + vite-plugin-singlefile 2.3.4 (inlines lazy chunks in the offline file), vitest 4.1.12, Playwright 1.64.1 + axe 4.14.0, TypeScript 5.9.3, ESLint 9 (no new dependency).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-18-tci-atlas-v2-design.md` — P2 section, catalog D1–D5, D7, E5, E11, R3, R4, "Journal registry", "Match my abstract v1", "Data contract v2" (modules `journals.json`, `diff.json`; pages self-hide by `manifest.modules`), Verification "P2+". Builds on `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-19-p1a-core-analytics.md` (contract, export, validate, publish, LLM runner) and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-19-p1bc-web-v2.md` (UI kit, DESIGN.md, fixtures, e2e). Branch `p2-editors` at `7164742` (= `main` after P1).

## Prerequisites (stop if any is false)

- `git log -1 --format=%h` on `p2-editors` prints `7164742` (or a descendant that only touched docs).
- From `pipeline/`: `uv run pytest -q -m "not slow"` is green; from `web/`: `npm run lint && npx tsc -b && npm test && npm run build && npm run e2e` is green.
- Never `git push`, create remotes or run `gh` without the owner's go-ahead.

## Global Constraints

Pipeline (copied from the P1a plan, still binding):
- Run from `pipeline/` with `uv run …`; Python ≥ 3.11 (CI 3.12); pandas 3 safe (never test `dtype == object`; normalise text with `.fillna("").astype(str)`; a nullable boolean mask is `.fillna(False).astype(bool)` before indexing); tables only through `atlas.io_utils.read_table` / `write_table`; strict JSON (no NaN/Infinity); persistent data only in `state_dir`, `work_dir` safe to delete; tests pass on Windows and Ubuntu; `uv run ruff check --fix .` before each commit; every commit ends with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`.
- **No hard-coded thresholds:** every result-changing number is a key in `AtlasConfig` **and** documented in `config.example.yaml` (`test_example_yaml_is_valid_and_documents_every_key` enforces it). New sections: `journals`, `methods`, `match`, `changes`; new keys `llm.tasks.methods`, `export.offline_exclude`, `validation.max_file_bytes`.
- **Emerging definition stays at `definition_version` 3.** No task touches `emerging.py`, `docs/EMERGING_SPEC.md` or a status rule.
- **Tests never touch the network** (the session fixture in `tests/conftest.py` blocks non-loopback sockets); LLMs are fakes with `model_id` + `chat(messages) -> atlas.llm.Reply`.
- **Thai text in code:** Thai words may be literal UTF-8; single Thai code points are written `\N{THAI CHARACTER …}` or `chr(0x0E..)`; **never** a backslash-u escape in Python, TypeScript or JavaScript (copying tools decode them into invisible characters — in JS build such characters with `String.fromCodePoint`).
- **Contract:** every published file is a pydantic model in `atlas/schema/contract.py`; after changing a model run `uv run atlas schema` (from `pipeline/`) and `npm run gen:types` (from `web/`) and commit both generated files. Colours are token keys, never hex.
- **LLM:** topic level only; no article-level calls. P2's only LLM work (`methods` notes) is OFF by default (`methods.provider: off`) and goes through `atlas.llm_runner.run_job` (cache, budget, stop, fallback) like every P1 task; the prompt lives only in `prompts/topic_methods.th.md`.

Web (copied from the P1b/P1c plan, still binding):
- Every command runs in `web/`; gate for every web task: `npm run lint && npx tsc -b && npm test && npm run build && npm run e2e`. Pins exact (no `^`/`~`), `package-lock.json` committed, no new dependency.
- Data: relative `${import.meta.env.BASE_URL}data/…` URLs only; types only from the generated `src/data/contract.ts` through `src/data/types.ts` (containers by name, nested types by indexed access); never hand-edit `contract.ts`; fields with pydantic defaults are optional — read them with `??`.
- HashRouter only; never an in-page `href="#id"`. No third-party requests at run time.
- DESIGN.md wins: colours only through CSS custom properties in `src/styles/tokens.css`; no hex in `.tsx`; no dynamic Tailwind classes; dynamic widths via `style={{ … }}`.
- Status encoding glyph + colour + text; Thai typography (no italics, no letter-spacing on Thai, line-height ≥ 1.5); bilingual strings in `src/strings/*.ts` (th + en, same placeholders; a key defined once — `i18n.test.ts`).
- Self-hiding sections; a page whose module is absent shows its `h1` and why (never a white page, never `role="alert"` for a missing module).
- Accessibility WCAG 2.2 AA: axe serious/critical = 0 in light and dark on every route; keyboard-reachable controls ≥ 24 px; sortable headers carry `aria-sort`; information never by colour alone.
- Lint rules that shape code: a `.tsx` exports components only (hooks/helpers in `.ts`); no synchronous `setState` in an effect body; no ref access during render; copy before `.sort()`; no `Date.now`/`Math.random` in render.
- UI tasks: the implementer invokes the `impeccable` skill (craft mode) before writing UI and follows DESIGN.md; charts follow the `dataviz` skill rules (reuse `MultiLineChart`, table view for every chart).

New in P2:
- **Privacy (PDPA):** author rows are read inside `journal_metrics` / `journals` only; published journal metrics are aggregates (counts, shares, effective number of institutions). No person's name, no per-author row, no ranking of people. "Foreign authors" = share of articles with ≥ 1 affiliation outside Thailand — never names.
- **No abstracts published:** methods and the match index read abstracts internally; only method counts/shares, sample-size quantiles and character-trigram weights leave the pipeline (`test_release_files_match_the_contract_and_link_back_to_thaijo` keeps scanning every published file).
- **Registry is optional:** every registry field is optional in the contract; the UI shows the OJS name when no registry name exists; an owner institution not given by the registry is **inferred and labelled "อนุมาน / inferred"**, never presented as fact.
- **The pasted abstract never leaves the browser:** not in the URL, not in storage, not in a request (`/match` e2e asserts the URL).
- **Bundle:** the five editors' pages are `React.lazy` routes; `node scripts/check-bundle.mjs` keeps the entry chunk ≤ 180 KB gzip and fails if a lazy page lands in the entry chunk. `match.json` is left out of the offline HTML (`export.offline_exclude`).

## Decisions this plan takes (controller: confirm or override)

1. **Journal identity** = `<server>:<OJS path>` from the article id (`so02:BECJournal`), published id `so02.BECJournal` (URL- and file-safe; non-ASCII keys → `J` + 12 hex of sha1). Stable across releases because it is a function of the key.
2. **Registry matching** accepts the full key or the bare OJS path when it is unique; duplicate keys, unknown columns or a tier outside 1–3 stop the step (`RegistryError` naming the row); unmatched rows are warnings. `pipeline/registry/` stays git-ignored except `*.example.csv`.
3. **Owner institution:** registry `publisher_org` (source `registry`) else the most frequent organisation on the journal's window articles when it signs ≥ `journals.owner_min_share` (20 %) of them (source `inferred`, labelled in the UI). In-house share = articles with ≥ 1 owner author ÷ articles with ≥ 1 recognised organisation.
4. **D2 completeness** = 7 elements (EN abstract, EN title, EN keywords, DOI, references, Thai references translated, every affiliation) weighted by config; the score is labelled "not the official TCI assessment" everywhere. `journals.inhouse_flag_share` defaults to **null** (no "above the bar" mark) — see controller question 1.
5. **Peers** = cosine of window topic-mix vectors (journals with ≥ `min_articles`); **scope drift** = Jensen–Shannon distance (base 2) of each year's mix vs the 3 previous years; **special issue** = EMERGING/WATCH topics hosted (≥ 3 articles in R) by ≤ 5 journals; per journal "in scope" (already publishes it) or "adjacent" (map edge to one of its top 5 topics).
6. **Methods** = 16-method TH/EN regex lexicon on folded text (tone marks dropped) + a sample-size reader; topics with ≥ 30 window articles are published; "monoculture" (E11) = share of method-naming articles that use only questionnaire + descriptive statistics, flag at ≥ 60 %. LLM notes: `methods.provider: off` by default; when on, ≤ 50 largest topics, grounded with `narrative.grounding_error`, template fallback.
7. **Match index** = character trigrams (scikit-learn `char_wb` semantics) on text folded exactly like the web search (`foldText`), up to 100 sampled articles per topic (title + keywords + first 600 characters of each abstract), top 150 trigrams per topic, shrinking until the file is ≤ 5 MB. Journals for R4 are ranked from their topic mix (no journal text vectors). The match index is left out of the offline HTML; `/match` there explains it needs the web edition.
8. **Quarter diff** is computed at export from `state_dir/releases/<prev>/snapshot.json` (written by every export from P2 on); a pre-P2 previous release falls back to `output/data/<prev>/` (`previous_source: output`, shown on the page); neither → no `diff.json`.
9. **Contract 2.1.0** (minor, additive): `JournalsFile`, `JournalDetail`, `MethodsFile`, `DiffFile`, `MatchIndex`, `CountItem.id` (topic pages link journals). The web keeps checking major 2 only.
10. **Navigation** grows to ten items (Journals, Methods, What changed, Match an abstract added after Topic status); the nav row already wraps — Task 17's impeccable pass may regroup it but must keep every page one click away.

Controller questions (one line each; the plan proceeds with the stated default):
- Q1 — TCI's in-house threshold for `journals.inhouse_flag_share`: default **null** (no mark) until TCI confirms the current criterion.
- Q2 — Name source for journals before the official registry: default **OJS name + optional local `registry/journals.csv`**; confirm no scraping of ThaiJO journal pages is wanted.

## Review Focus

1. **A journal with no registry row and few articles** (most of 1–2K journals at first) → listed by its OJS name, every rate null with "บทความน้อย / Few articles", never an empty title or a 0 % that looks like a fact — Task 2 (`low_data`), Task 6 (`test_summary_has_identity_counts_and_rates`), Task 12 e2e (`example2`, `jr.lowData`).
2. **An inferred owner is wrong** (a big co-authoring university outnumbers the real owner) → shown as "อนุมาน / inferred" next to the share on the list and with the sentence "inferred from the articles — not in the registry" on the journal page; a registry owner never found among authors is still used (`owner_matched: false`) — Task 2 (`test_owner_from_registry_or_inferred_and_labelled`), Task 12/13 e2e.
3. **Thai text damaged by PDF conversion** (tone marks lost, decomposed sara am, zero-width spaces) in an abstract or a pasted query → methods and the matcher fold it the same way as the web search — Task 4 (`test_thai_and_english_methods_are_found_even_without_tone_marks`), Task 7 + Task 16 (shared `match_parity.json`).
4. **Opening the offline HTML or an older (pre-P2) release** → `/journals`, `/methods`, `/changes`, `/match` show their `h1` and why (module absent / offline edition / no previous release), no alert banner, no console error — Task 11 (`ModuleFile`, release tests), Task 16 (offline-without-match e2e), empty-release e2e on every new route.
5. **A previous release exported before P2** (no snapshot in `state_dir`) → the diff falls back to its published folder and says so; a missing previous release just leaves `/changes` explaining — Task 8 (`test_fallback_reads_the_previous_release_folder`), Task 15 (`ch.fromOutput`).
6. **Corpus scale (500K articles, 2K journals)** → no per-journal full-table filter (one `groupby` pass), match index sampled per topic and size-bounded, journal detail files small — Task 2/6 code, Task 7 (`test_the_index_is_size_bounded_and_carries_no_text`), Task 10 (`validation.max_file_bytes`).
7. **Someone pastes a long confidential abstract** → it stays in component state only; the URL never changes with it; nothing is fetched except the static index — Task 16 e2e.

### Task 1: Journal identity, the optional registry and the `journals` config

Journals exist only in the corpus today (`journal_name` = OJS path). This task gives every journal a stable key and a URL/file-safe id, loads the optional registry with clear errors, and adds every journal threshold to the config. No step runs it yet (Task 6).

**Files:**
- Create: `pipeline/atlas/registry.py`, `pipeline/registry/journals.example.csv`, `pipeline/tests/test_registry.py`
- Modify: `pipeline/atlas/config.py`, `pipeline/config.example.yaml`, `.gitignore`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `atlas.thai.clean_text`, `atlas.thai.norm_key`.
- Produces: `atlas.registry` — `REGISTRY_COLUMNS`, `RegistryError(ValueError)`, `journal_key(article_id, journal_name) -> str`, `journal_keys(df) -> pd.Series`, `journal_code(key) -> str`, `journal_path(key) -> str`, `load_registry(path) -> (pd.DataFrame, list[str])` (`tci_tier` nullable Int64), `match_registry(keys, registry) -> (dict[key, row dict], list[str])`. `atlas.config` — `COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS` (7 names), `DEFAULT_COMPLETENESS_WEIGHTS`, `JournalsCfg` (`cfg["journals"]`, `registry_path` resolved against the config file).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — create `pipeline/tests/test_registry.py`:

```python
"""Journal identity from the corpus and the optional registry/journals.csv."""
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd
import pytest

from atlas.config import AtlasConfig, load_config
from atlas.registry import (
    REGISTRY_COLUMNS,
    RegistryError,
    journal_code,
    journal_key,
    journal_keys,
    load_registry,
    match_registry,
)

PIPELINE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent


def test_journal_key_comes_from_the_article_id_and_falls_back_to_the_name():
    assert journal_key("so02:BECJournal:74136", "BECJournal") == "so02:BECJournal"
    assert journal_key("file:0a1b2c", "Journal of Things ") == "name:journalofthings"
    assert journal_key("", "") == ""
    df = pd.DataFrame({"article_id": ["so05:jedu:1", "x"], "journal_name": ["jedu", "วารสาร ก"]})
    assert journal_keys(df).tolist() == ["so05:jedu", "name:วารสารก"]


def test_journal_code_is_url_safe_and_stable():
    assert journal_code("so02:BECJournal") == "so02.BECJournal"
    thai = journal_code("name:วารสารก")
    assert thai.startswith("J") and len(thai) == 13 and thai == journal_code("name:วารสารก")
    assert journal_code("so02:a b") != journal_code("so02:a_b")


def test_missing_registry_is_empty(tmp_path):
    df, warns = load_registry(tmp_path / "none.csv")
    assert list(df.columns) == REGISTRY_COLUMNS and df.empty and warns == []
    assert load_registry(None)[0].empty


def test_registry_loads_excel_csv_with_bom_and_optional_columns(tmp_path):
    p = tmp_path / "journals.csv"
    p.write_text("journal_key,name_th,issn,tci_tier\nso02:BECJournal,วารสาร ก,19059566,กลุ่ม 2\nBECX, ,,\n",
                 encoding="utf-8-sig")
    df, warns = load_registry(p)
    row = df.set_index("journal_key").loc["so02:BECJournal"]
    assert row["name_th"] == "วารสาร ก" and row["issn"] == "1905-9566" and int(row["tci_tier"]) == 2
    assert pd.isna(df.set_index("journal_key").loc["BECX", "tci_tier"]) and row["url"] == ""
    assert warns == []


@pytest.mark.parametrize("body, message", [
    ("name_th\nก\n", "journal_key column"),
    ("journal_key,owner\na,b\n", "unknown columns"),
    ("journal_key\na\na\n", "more than once"),
    ("journal_key,tci_tier\na,4\n", "row 2: tci_tier"),
])
def test_registry_errors_name_the_problem(tmp_path, body, message):
    p = tmp_path / "j.csv"
    p.write_text(body, encoding="utf-8")
    with pytest.raises(RegistryError, match=message):
        load_registry(p)


def test_a_bad_issn_is_kept_with_a_warning(tmp_path):
    p = tmp_path / "j.csv"
    p.write_text("journal_key,issn\na,12-34\n", encoding="utf-8")
    df, warns = load_registry(p)
    assert df.loc[0, "issn"] == "12-34" and "row 2" in warns[0]


def test_match_on_full_key_or_unique_bare_path():
    reg = pd.DataFrame([{**dict.fromkeys(REGISTRY_COLUMNS, ""), "journal_key": k}
                        for k in ("so02:A", "B", "C", "nowhere")])
    matched, warns = match_registry(["so02:A", "so03:B", "so04:C", "so05:C"], reg)
    assert set(matched) == {"so02:A", "so03:B"}
    assert any("match no journal" in w for w in warns) and any("several servers" in w for w in warns)
    two = pd.DataFrame([{**dict.fromkeys(REGISTRY_COLUMNS, ""), "journal_key": k} for k in ("so03:B", "B")])
    with pytest.raises(RegistryError, match="same journal"):
        match_registry(["so03:B"], two)


def test_example_registry_loads_and_config_resolves_its_path(tmp_path):
    df, warns = load_registry(PIPELINE / "registry" / "journals.example.csv")
    assert len(df) >= 1 and warns == []
    p = tmp_path / "c.yaml"
    p.write_text("journals:\n  registry_path: ./reg/j.csv\n", encoding="utf-8")
    assert Path(load_config(p)["journals"]["registry_path"]) == (tmp_path / "reg" / "j.csv").resolve()
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="unknown completeness components"):
        AtlasConfig.model_validate({"journals": {"completeness_weights": {"pages": 1.0}}})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `pipeline/atlas/registry.py`**

```python
"""Journal identity from the corpus + the optional journal registry (registry/journals.csv).

The official TCI journal list is not available yet, so journals come from the corpus:
  journal_key   "<server>:<OJS path>" from the article id ("so02:BECJournal:74136" → "so02:BECJournal");
                records without such an id (table input) → "name:<normalised journal name>"
  journal_code  the published id (URL path and file name): "so02.BECJournal", or "J" + 12 hex digits of
                sha1(key) when the key is not ASCII-safe — a fixed function of the key, so stable across releases
registry/journals.csv (git-ignored; registry/journals.example.csv shows the columns) adds names, ISSN, TCI
tier/field, publisher and URL. Every column except journal_key is optional and may be empty. A row matches a
corpus journal by its full key, or by the bare OJS path when exactly one corpus journal has that path.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import re
from collections.abc import Iterable
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd

from atlas.thai import clean_text, norm_key

REGISTRY_COLUMNS = ["journal_key", "name_th", "name_en", "issn", "eissn", "tci_tier", "tci_field",
                    "publisher_org", "url"]
_ARTICLE_ID = re.compile(r"^(?P<host>[A-Za-z0-9_-]+):(?P<path>[^:\s]+):\d+$")
_SAFE = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,80}$")
_ISSN = re.compile(r"^\d{4}-?\d{3}[\dX]$")
_TIER_WORDS = re.compile(r"(?i)กลุ่ม|tier|group")


class RegistryError(ValueError):
    """registry/journals.csv cannot be used as written (the message names the row)."""


def journal_key(article_id: str, journal_name: str) -> str:
    m = _ARTICLE_ID.match(str(article_id or ""))
    if m:
        return f"{m['host']}:{m['path']}"
    name = norm_key(str(journal_name or ""))[:60]
    return f"name:{name}" if name else ""


def journal_keys(articles: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """journal_key of every row of a table with article_id and journal_name."""
    ids = articles["article_id"].fillna("").astype(str)
    names = articles["journal_name"].fillna("").astype(str) if "journal_name" in articles else pd.Series(
        "", index=articles.index)
    return pd.Series([journal_key(a, j) for a, j in zip(ids, names)], index=articles.index, dtype=str)


def journal_code(key: str) -> str:
    cand = key.replace(":", ".", 1)
    if ":" not in cand and _SAFE.fullmatch(cand):
        return cand
    return "J" + hashlib.sha1(key.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:12]


def journal_path(key: str) -> str:
    """The OJS path of a key ("so02:BECJournal" → "BECJournal"); the normalised name for "name:" keys."""
    return key.split(":", 1)[1] if ":" in key else key


def _issn(value: str, where: str, warnings: list[str]) -> str:
    v = value.strip().upper().replace(" ", "")
    if not v:
        return ""
    if not _ISSN.fullmatch(v):
        warnings.append(f"{where}: ISSN {value!r} is not NNNN-NNNC — kept as written")
        return value.strip()
    return v if "-" in v else f"{v[:4]}-{v[4:]}"


def load_registry(path: str | Path | None) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, list[str]]:
    """(rows with REGISTRY_COLUMNS — tci_tier as nullable Int64, text cleaned —, warnings).
    No path or no file → an empty table (the registry is optional). Raises RegistryError for a file that
    cannot be trusted: no journal_key column, unknown columns, a duplicated key, a tier other than 1–3."""
    if not path or not Path(path).exists():
        return pd.DataFrame({c: pd.Series(dtype="Int64" if c == "tci_tier" else str) for c in REGISTRY_COLUMNS}), []
    raw = pd.read_csv(path, dtype=str, keep_default_na=False, encoding="utf-8-sig")   # Excel saves a BOM
    raw.columns = [str(c).strip() for c in raw.columns]
    if "journal_key" not in raw.columns:
        raise RegistryError(f"{path}: the header needs a journal_key column (columns: {REGISTRY_COLUMNS})")
    unknown = sorted(set(raw.columns) - set(REGISTRY_COLUMNS))
    if unknown:
        raise RegistryError(f"{path}: unknown columns {unknown} (allowed: {REGISTRY_COLUMNS})")
    df = raw.reindex(columns=REGISTRY_COLUMNS, fill_value="")
    for c in REGISTRY_COLUMNS:
        df[c] = df[c].map(clean_text)
    df = df[df["journal_key"] != ""]
    dup = df["journal_key"][df["journal_key"].duplicated()]
    if len(dup):
        raise RegistryError(f"{path}: journal_key {dup.iloc[0]!r} appears more than once")
    warnings: list[str] = []
    tiers = []
    for i, v in zip(df.index, df["tci_tier"]):
        t = _TIER_WORDS.sub("", v).strip()
        if t == "":
            tiers.append(pd.NA)
        elif t in ("1", "2", "3"):
            tiers.append(int(t))
        else:
            raise RegistryError(f"{path} row {i + 2}: tci_tier {v!r} must be 1, 2 or 3 (or empty)")
    df["tci_tier"] = pd.array(tiers, dtype="Int64")
    for col in ("issn", "eissn"):
        df[col] = [_issn(v, f"{path} row {i + 2}", warnings) for i, v in zip(df.index, df[col])]
    return df.reset_index(drop=True), warnings


def match_registry(keys: Iterable[str], registry: pd.DataFrame) -> tuple[dict[str, dict], list[str]]:
    """{corpus journal key: registry row as a dict} + warnings for rows that match no (or no single) journal."""
    known = sorted(set(keys))
    by_path: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    for k in known:
        if not k.startswith("name:"):
            by_path.setdefault(journal_path(k), []).append(k)
    out: dict[str, dict] = {}
    unmatched, ambiguous = [], []
    known_set = set(known)
    for row in registry.to_dict("records"):
        rk = row["journal_key"]
        if rk in known_set:
            target = rk
        elif len(by_path.get(rk, [])) == 1:
            target = by_path[rk][0]
        else:
            (ambiguous if len(by_path.get(rk, [])) > 1 else unmatched).append(rk)
            continue
        if target in out:
            raise RegistryError(f"registry rows {out[target]['journal_key']!r} and {rk!r} name the same journal "
                                f"{target!r}")
        out[target] = row
    warnings = []
    if unmatched:
        warnings.append(f"{len(unmatched)} registry rows match no journal in the corpus (e.g. {unmatched[0]!r})")
    if ambiguous:
        warnings.append(f"{len(ambiguous)} registry rows name an OJS path used on several servers — write the full "
                        f"key <server>:<path> (e.g. {ambiguous[0]!r})")
    return out, warnings
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the config section.** In `pipeline/atlas/config.py` insert the block below (followed by two blank lines) directly above the line `class ExportCfg(_Strict):`:

```python
COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS = ("abstract_en", "title_en", "keywords_en", "doi", "references", "refs_romanized",
                           "affiliations")
DEFAULT_COMPLETENESS_WEIGHTS = {"abstract_en": 0.20, "title_en": 0.10, "keywords_en": 0.15, "doi": 0.20,
                                "references": 0.15, "refs_romanized": 0.10, "affiliations": 0.10}


class JournalsCfg(_Strict):
    """Editors' journal module (P2): metrics per journal over the last window_years complete years."""
    registry_path: str | None = None      # registry/journals.csv (optional); the example config sets ./registry/…
    window_years: int = Field(5, ge=1)
    min_articles: int = Field(20, ge=1)   # fewer articles in the window → rates are null (low_data)
    completeness_weights: dict[str, float] = Field(default_factory=lambda: dict(DEFAULT_COMPLETENESS_WEIGHTS))
    inhouse_flag_share: float | None = Field(None, gt=0.0, le=1.0)   # the web marks shares above it; null = no mark
    owner_min_share: float = Field(0.2, ge=0.0, le=1.0)   # an inferred owner must sign ≥ this share of the articles
    peers_k: int = Field(8, ge=0)
    peers_min_similarity: float = Field(0.2, ge=0.0, le=1.0)
    top_topics: int = Field(15, ge=1)
    drift_reference_years: int = Field(3, ge=1)
    drift_min_articles: int = Field(10, ge=1)
    shift_topics: int = Field(5, ge=0)
    special_issue_statuses: list[Literal["EMERGING", "WATCH", "HOT"]] = Field(
        default_factory=lambda: ["EMERGING", "WATCH"])
    special_issue_min_host_articles: int = Field(3, ge=1)
    special_issue_max_hosts: int = Field(5, ge=0)
    special_issue_per_journal: int = Field(5, ge=0)
    scope_topics: int = Field(5, ge=1)    # a topic next to one of the journal's top N topics is "adjacent"

    @field_validator("completeness_weights")
    @classmethod
    def _known_components(cls, v: dict[str, float]) -> dict[str, float]:
        unknown = sorted(set(v) - set(COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS))
        if unknown:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown completeness components {unknown}; choose from {list(COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS)}")
        if any(w < 0 for w in v.values()) or sum(v.values()) <= 0:
            raise ValueError("completeness weights must be >= 0 and sum to more than 0")
        return v
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Document every key.** In `pipeline/config.example.yaml` insert the block below (followed by one blank line) directly above the line `export:`:

```yaml
# ---------- P2 บรรณาธิการ: วารสาร (ระดับวารสาร/สถาบันเท่านั้น — ไม่มีข้อมูลรายบุคคล) ----------
journals:
  registry_path: ./registry/journals.csv   # ไม่บังคับ: ชื่อเต็ม/ISSN/tier/สถาบันเจ้าของ (คอลัมน์ดู registry/journals.example.csv) — ไม่มีไฟล์ = แสดงชื่อ OJS
  window_years: 5             # ตัวชี้วัดวารสารคิดจาก 5 ปีล่าสุดที่ข้อมูลครบ
  min_articles: 20            # วารสารที่มีบทความในช่วงนั้น < 20 เรื่อง → ไม่คำนวณสัดส่วน (ข้อมูลน้อย)
  completeness_weights:       # D2 คะแนนความครบถ้วน metadata 0–100 = ค่าเฉลี่ยถ่วงน้ำหนักของอัตราต่อองค์ประกอบ (ไม่ใช่ผลประเมิน TCI ทางการ)
    abstract_en: 0.20         # มีบทคัดย่อภาษาอังกฤษ
    title_en: 0.10            # มีชื่อเรื่องภาษาอังกฤษ
    keywords_en: 0.15         # มีคำสำคัญภาษาอังกฤษ
    doi: 0.20                 # มี DOI
    references: 0.15          # มีรายการอ้างอิง (ไม่ใช่มีแค่ "วิธีอ้างอิงบทความนี้")
    refs_romanized: 0.10      # บทความที่อ้างเอกสารภาษาไทย มีรายการอ้างอิงแปลเป็นภาษาอังกฤษ (Translated Thai References)
    affiliations: 0.10        # ผู้แต่งทุกคนระบุสังกัด
  inhouse_flag_share: null    # เว็บทำเครื่องหมายวารสารที่สัดส่วนบทความจากสถาบันเจ้าของ > ค่านี้ (ใส่ตามเกณฑ์ TCI รอบปัจจุบัน) — null = ไม่ทำเครื่องหมาย
  owner_min_share: 0.2        # ไม่มีสถาบันเจ้าของใน registry → อนุมานจากสถาบันที่พบบ่อยที่สุด (ติดป้าย "อนุมาน") เฉพาะเมื่อพบใน ≥ 20% ของบทความ
  peers_k: 8                  # D4 วารสารคู่เทียบสูงสุดต่อวารสาร
  peers_min_similarity: 0.2   # cosine ของสัดส่วนหัวข้อ ≥ ค่านี้
  top_topics: 15              # หัวข้อที่แสดงต่อวารสาร
  drift_reference_years: 3    # D1 scope drift: เทียบสัดส่วนหัวข้อของแต่ละปีกับ 3 ปีก่อนหน้า (Jensen–Shannon distance 0–1)
  drift_min_articles: 10      # ปีที่มีบทความ < 10 เรื่อง (หรือช่วงอ้างอิง < 10) ไม่คำนวณ drift
  shift_topics: 5             # หัวข้อที่สัดส่วนเพิ่ม/ลดมากที่สุด (ช่วง R เทียบช่วง B ของ emerging) ที่แสดงต่อวารสาร
  special_issue_statuses: [EMERGING, WATCH]   # D5 สถานะหัวข้อที่อาจเปิดฉบับพิเศษ
  special_issue_min_host_articles: 3   # วารสารที่ตีพิมพ์หัวข้อนั้น ≥ 3 เรื่องในช่วง R = "รองรับ" หัวข้อนั้น
  special_issue_max_hosts: 5  # หัวข้อที่มีวารสารรองรับ ≤ 5 วารสาร = โอกาสฉบับพิเศษ
  special_issue_per_journal: 5   # จำนวนโอกาสที่แสดงต่อวารสาร
  scope_topics: 5             # หัวข้อที่ติดกับ (edge บนแผนที่) หัวข้อหลัก 5 อันดับแรกของวารสาร = "ใกล้ขอบเขต"
```

### Task 2: D2 metadata completeness and D3 owner, in-house share, diversity and foreign authors

Pure functions over the ingest tables (`01_articles`, `01_authors`, `01_references`), aggregates per journal only. Foreign affiliations come from a small country lexicon tuned for precision (a Thai department of Chinese language must not count as foreign).

**Files:**
- Create: `pipeline/atlas/countries.py`, `pipeline/atlas/journal_metrics.py`, `pipeline/tests/test_journal_metrics.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `atlas.config.COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS`, `atlas.orgs.org_key`, `atlas.thai.clean_text`, `atlas.thai.thai_ratio`.
- Produces: `atlas.countries.is_foreign_affiliation(th, en) -> bool`; `atlas.journal_metrics` — `article_completeness(arts, refs, authors) -> DataFrame` (article_id + 7 nullable-boolean columns), `completeness_table(comp, journal_of, weights) -> (long DataFrame journal_key/component/rate/n, Series score 0–100)`, `owner_key_of(name) -> str`, `owners(arts, authors, registry_owner, min_share, display) -> DataFrame[OWNER_COLUMNS]` (journal_key, owner_key, owner_name, owner_source ∈ {registry, inferred, None}, owner_share, owner_matched), `author_metrics(arts, authors, owner_df) -> DataFrame[AUTHOR_METRIC_COLUMNS]` (journal_key, with_org, affiliation_coverage, inhouse_share, n_orgs, effective_orgs, foreign_share). `arts` must carry `journal_key`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — create `pipeline/tests/test_journal_metrics.py`:

```python
"""D2 metadata completeness and D3 in-house / diversity / foreign authors — aggregates per journal."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pytest

from atlas.config import DEFAULT_COMPLETENESS_WEIGHTS
from atlas.countries import is_foreign_affiliation
from atlas.journal_metrics import (
    article_completeness,
    author_metrics,
    completeness_table,
    owner_key_of,
    owners,
)
from atlas.orgs import org_key

NU = "คณะบริหารธุรกิจ มหาวิทยาลัยนเรศวร"
CU = "คณะครุศาสตร์ จุฬาลงกรณ์มหาวิทยาลัย"
KU = "มหาวิทยาลัยเกษตรศาสตร์"


def _arts():
    return pd.DataFrame({
        "article_id": ["a1", "a2", "a3", "a4", "b1"],
        "journal_key": ["so02:J1", "so02:J1", "so02:J1", "so02:J1", "so03:J2"],
        "abstract_en": ["x", "", "x", "x", "x"], "title_en": ["t", "t", "t", "", "t"],
        "keywords_en": ["k", "k", "", "", "k"], "doi": ["10.1/a", "", "", "", "10.1/b"],
        "has_references": [True, True, False, True, True], "self_citation_only": [False, True, False, False, False]})


def _authors():
    rows = [("a1", NU, ""), ("a1", "", "University of Tokyo, Japan"), ("a2", NU, ""), ("a3", CU, ""),
            ("a4", "", ""), ("b1", KU, "")]
    df = pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=["article_id", "affiliation_th", "affiliation_en"])
    return df.assign(org_key=[org_key(t, e) for t, e in zip(df["affiliation_th"], df["affiliation_en"])])


def _refs():
    return pd.DataFrame({"article_id": ["a1", "a1", "a2", "a3"], "lang": ["th", "latin", "th", "latin"],
                         "translated": [False, True, False, False]})


@pytest.mark.parametrize("th, en, foreign", [
    ("", "University of Tokyo, Japan", True),
    ("มหาวิทยาลัยแห่งชาติลาว สปป.ลาว", "", True),
    ("ภาควิชาภาษาจีน คณะมนุษยศาสตร์ มหาวิทยาลัยเชียงใหม่", "", False),     # "จีน" without "ประเทศ"
    ("", "Japanese Studies Center, Thammasat University", False),
    ("", "Asian Institute of Technology, Pathum Thani, Thailand", False),
    ("มหาวิทยาลัยโตเกียว ประเทศญี่ปุ่น", "", True),
    ("", "Cardiff University, UK", True),
    ("", "", False),
])
def test_foreign_affiliations(th, en, foreign):
    assert is_foreign_affiliation(th, en) is foreign


def test_article_completeness_marks_unknown_elements_as_missing_data():
    comp = article_completeness(_arts(), _refs(), _authors()).set_index("article_id")
    assert comp.loc["a1", "doi"] and not comp.loc["a2", "doi"]
    assert not comp.loc["a2", "references"]                         # only "how to cite this article"
    assert comp.loc["a1", "refs_romanized"] and not comp.loc["a2", "refs_romanized"]
    assert pd.isna(comp.loc["a3", "refs_romanized"])                # no Thai-language references
    assert not comp.loc["a4", "affiliations"] and comp.loc["a1", "affiliations"]
    assert pd.isna(article_completeness(_arts(), _refs(), _authors().iloc[0:0]).loc[0, "affiliations"])


def test_completeness_score_is_a_weighted_mean_of_known_rates():
    arts = _arts()
    comp = article_completeness(arts, _refs(), _authors())
    long, score = completeness_table(comp, arts.set_index("article_id")["journal_key"], DEFAULT_COMPLETENESS_WEIGHTS)
    j1 = long[long["journal_key"] == "so02:J1"].set_index("component")
    assert j1.loc["doi", "rate"] == pytest.approx(0.25) and j1.loc["doi", "n"] == 4
    assert j1.loc["refs_romanized", "n"] == 2 and j1.loc["refs_romanized", "rate"] == pytest.approx(0.5)
    w = DEFAULT_COMPLETENESS_WEIGHTS
    expected = 100 * sum(w[c] * j1.loc[c, "rate"] for c in w) / sum(w.values())
    assert score["so02:J1"] == pytest.approx(round(expected, 1))
    j2 = long[long["journal_key"] == "so03:J2"].set_index("component")
    assert np.isnan(j2.loc["refs_romanized", "rate"])               # J2 cites no Thai sources: weight dropped
    known = {c: w[c] for c in w if c != "refs_romanized"}
    assert score["so03:J2"] == pytest.approx(round(100 * sum(known[c] * j2.loc[c, "rate"] for c in known)
                                                   / sum(known.values()), 1))


def test_owner_from_registry_or_inferred_and_labelled():
    arts, auth = _arts(), _authors()
    display = {org_key(NU, ""): "มหาวิทยาลัยนเรศวร"}
    inferred = owners(arts, auth, {}, 0.2, display).set_index("journal_key")
    assert inferred.loc["so02:J1", "owner_source"] == "inferred"
    assert inferred.loc["so02:J1", "owner_name"] == "มหาวิทยาลัยนเรศวร"
    assert inferred.loc["so02:J1", "owner_share"] == pytest.approx(2 / 3)    # a4 has no recognised organisation
    reg = owners(arts, auth, {"so02:J1": "จุฬาลงกรณ์มหาวิทยาลัย"}, 0.2, display).set_index("journal_key")
    assert reg.loc["so02:J1", "owner_source"] == "registry" and reg.loc["so02:J1", "owner_matched"]
    assert reg.loc["so02:J1", "owner_share"] == pytest.approx(1 / 3)
    strict = owners(arts, auth, {}, 0.9, display).set_index("journal_key")
    assert pd.isna(strict.loc["so02:J1", "owner_source"]) and strict.loc["so02:J1", "owner_key"] == ""
    assert owner_key_of("Chulalongkorn University") == org_key("", "Chulalongkorn University")


def test_inhouse_diversity_and_foreign_shares():
    arts, auth = _arts(), _authors()
    own = owners(arts, auth, {}, 0.2, {})
    m = author_metrics(arts, auth, own).set_index("journal_key")
    j1 = m.loc["so02:J1"]
    assert j1["with_org"] == 3 and j1["affiliation_coverage"] == pytest.approx(0.75)
    assert j1["inhouse_share"] == pytest.approx(2 / 3)
    assert j1["n_orgs"] == 3                                    # NU, Tokyo, CU
    p = np.array([2, 1, 1]) / 4
    assert j1["effective_orgs"] == pytest.approx(1 / (p ** 2).sum())
    assert j1["foreign_share"] == pytest.approx(1 / 3)
    none = author_metrics(arts, auth.iloc[0:0], owners(arts, auth.iloc[0:0], {}, 0.2, {})).set_index("journal_key")
    assert none.loc["so02:J1", "with_org"] == 0 and np.isnan(none.loc["so02:J1", "inhouse_share"])
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `pipeline/atlas/countries.py`**

```python
"""Is an affiliation outside Thailand? A small country lexicon (D3 "foreign authors") until P3 adds ROR.

Rule (precision first — a Thai institution must never count as foreign):
  * an affiliation that names Thailand ("Thailand", "ประเทศไทย") is Thai;
  * Thai text names a foreign country only with the word "ประเทศ" (or สปป.ลาว), because bare names such as
    "จีน" also name Thai departments ("ภาควิชาภาษาจีน");
  * English text names a foreign country as a whole word ("Japan", not "Japanese Studies").
Only the share of articles with at least one such affiliation is published — never names.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import re

from atlas.thai import clean_text

COUNTRIES_EN = (
    "Australia", "Austria", "Bangladesh", "Belgium", "Bhutan", "Brazil", "Brunei", "Cambodia", "Canada", "China",
    "Denmark", "Egypt", "England", "Finland", "France", "Germany", "Hong Kong", "India", "Indonesia", "Iran",
    "Italy", "Japan", "Kenya", "Korea", "Lao PDR", "Laos", "Macau", "Malaysia", "Mongolia", "Myanmar", "Nepal",
    "Netherlands", "New Zealand", "Nigeria", "Norway", "Pakistan", "Philippines", "Poland", "Russia",
    "Saudi Arabia", "Scotland", "Singapore", "South Africa", "Spain", "Sri Lanka", "Sweden", "Switzerland",
    "Taiwan", "Timor-Leste", "Turkey", "United Kingdom", "United States", "USA", "U.S.A.", "UK", "Viet Nam",
    "Vietnam",
)
COUNTRIES_TH = (
    "ญี่ปุ่น", "จีน", "เกาหลีใต้", "เกาหลี", "ลาว", "กัมพูชา", "เวียดนาม", "เมียนมา", "พม่า", "มาเลเซีย", "อินโดนีเซีย",
    "ฟิลิปปินส์", "สิงคโปร์", "บรูไน", "อินเดีย", "ภูฏาน", "เนปาล", "บังกลาเทศ", "ศรีลังกา", "ปากีสถาน", "ไต้หวัน",
    "ออสเตรเลีย", "นิวซีแลนด์", "สหรัฐอเมริกา", "อังกฤษ", "สหราชอาณาจักร", "เยอรมนี", "ฝรั่งเศส", "เนเธอร์แลนด์",
    "สวีเดน", "แคนาดา",
)
_EN = re.compile(r"(?<![\w.])(?:" + "|".join(re.escape(c) for c in sorted(COUNTRIES_EN, key=len, reverse=True))
                 + r")(?![\w])")
_TH = re.compile("ประเทศ\\s*(?:" + "|".join(sorted(COUNTRIES_TH, key=len, reverse=True)) + ")|สปป\\.?\\s*ลาว")
_THAILAND = re.compile(r"(?i)\bthailand\b|ประเทศไทย")


def is_foreign_affiliation(affiliation_th: str | None, affiliation_en: str | None) -> bool:
    th, en = clean_text(affiliation_th), clean_text(affiliation_en)
    if _THAILAND.search(th) or _THAILAND.search(en):
        return False
    return bool(_TH.search(th) or _EN.search(en))
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Create `pipeline/atlas/journal_metrics.py`**

```python
"""Journal metrics for editors (D2 metadata completeness, D3 in-house / diversity / foreign authors).

Aggregates per journal only — author rows are read internally and never published (PDPA).

completeness     per metadata element (config.COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS) the share of a journal's window articles
                 that have it, and a 0–100 score = weighted mean of the known rates (journals.completeness_weights,
                 renormalised over the elements that have a rate). The elements follow the TCI assessment's
                 metadata items; the score is NOT the official TCI assessment.
                 refs_romanized is known only for articles that cite Thai-language sources; affiliations only
                 for articles with author rows.
owner            registry publisher_org (source "registry"), else the organisation on most articles (source
                 "inferred", only when it signs ≥ journals.owner_min_share of them) — inferences are labelled.
inhouse_share    share of articles with ≥ 1 author from the owner, among articles with ≥ 1 recognised organisation
effective_orgs   1 / Σ p² of the organisations' article shares ("as diverse as N equal institutions")
foreign_share    share of articles with ≥ 1 affiliation outside Thailand (atlas.countries), same denominator
affiliation_coverage  share of window articles with ≥ 1 recognised organisation (that denominator)
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from atlas.config import COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS
from atlas.countries import is_foreign_affiliation
from atlas.orgs import org_key
from atlas.thai import thai_ratio

AUTHOR_METRIC_COLUMNS = ["journal_key", "with_org", "affiliation_coverage", "inhouse_share", "n_orgs",
                         "effective_orgs", "foreign_share"]
OWNER_COLUMNS = ["journal_key", "owner_key", "owner_name", "owner_source", "owner_share", "owner_matched"]


def _text(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> pd.Series:
    return df[col].fillna("").astype(str).str.strip() if col in df.columns else pd.Series("", index=df.index)


def _flag(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> pd.Series:
    return df[col].fillna(False).astype(bool) if col in df.columns else pd.Series(False, index=df.index)


def article_completeness(arts: pd.DataFrame, refs: pd.DataFrame, authors: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """article_id + one nullable-boolean column per component (NA = not applicable / unknown)."""
    out = pd.DataFrame({"article_id": arts["article_id"].to_numpy()})
    for col in ("abstract_en", "title_en", "keywords_en", "doi"):
        out[col] = pd.array((_text(arts, col) != "").to_numpy(), dtype="boolean")
    out["references"] = pd.array((_flag(arts, "has_references") & ~_flag(arts, "self_citation_only")).to_numpy(),
                                 dtype="boolean")
    ids = out["article_id"]
    thai_refs = set(refs.loc[_text(refs, "lang") == "th", "article_id"]) if len(refs) else set()
    translated = set(refs.loc[_flag(refs, "translated"), "article_id"]) if len(refs) else set()
    out["refs_romanized"] = pd.array([(a in translated) if a in thai_refs else pd.NA for a in ids], dtype="boolean")
    if len(authors):
        has = (_text(authors, "affiliation_th") != "") | (_text(authors, "affiliation_en") != "")
        every = has.groupby(authors["article_id"]).all()
        out["affiliations"] = pd.array([bool(every[a]) if a in every.index else pd.NA for a in ids], dtype="boolean")
    else:
        out["affiliations"] = pd.array([pd.NA] * len(out), dtype="boolean")
    return out[["article_id", *COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS]]


def completeness_table(comp: pd.DataFrame, journal_of: pd.Series, weights: dict[str, float]
                       ) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, pd.Series]:
    """(long table journal_key, component, rate, n — rate null when n = 0; score per journal 0–100 or NaN).
    journal_of: article_id → journal_key."""
    c = comp.assign(journal_key=comp["article_id"].map(journal_of)).dropna(subset=["journal_key"])
    rows = []
    for col in COMPLETENESS_COMPONENTS:
        v = c[col].astype("Float64")
        g = v.groupby(c["journal_key"])
        rate, n = g.mean(), g.count()
        rows.append(pd.DataFrame({"journal_key": n.index, "component": col, "rate": rate.reindex(n.index).to_numpy(
            dtype=float, na_value=np.nan), "n": n.to_numpy(dtype=int)}))
    long = pd.concat(rows, ignore_index=True) if rows else pd.DataFrame(columns=["journal_key", "component", "rate", "n"])
    w = long["component"].map(weights).fillna(0.0)
    known = long["rate"].notna() & (w > 0)
    num = (long["rate"].where(known, 0.0) * w).groupby(long["journal_key"]).sum()
    den = w.where(known, 0.0).groupby(long["journal_key"]).sum()
    score = (100 * num / den.replace(0.0, np.nan)).round(1)
    return long, score


def owner_key_of(name: str) -> str:
    """org_key of a registry publisher name (Thai or English as written)."""
    name = name or ""
    return org_key(name, "") if thai_ratio(name) > 0.5 else org_key("", name)


def _orgs(arts: pd.DataFrame, authors: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Distinct (article_id, journal_key, org_key) for articles of `arts` (author rows with an org_key)."""
    if not len(authors):
        return pd.DataFrame(columns=["article_id", "journal_key", "org_key"])
    a = authors.loc[_text(authors, "org_key") != "", ["article_id", "org_key"]].drop_duplicates()
    return a.merge(arts[["article_id", "journal_key"]], on="article_id")


def owners(arts: pd.DataFrame, authors: pd.DataFrame, registry_owner: dict[str, str], min_share: float,
           display: dict[str, str]) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """One row per journal of `arts`: the owner organisation, how it is known, and the share of the journal's
    articles (with a recognised organisation) that it signs. display: org_key → written name."""
    orgs = _orgs(arts, authors)
    with_org = orgs.groupby("journal_key")["article_id"].nunique()
    per = orgs.groupby(["journal_key", "org_key"])["article_id"].nunique().rename("n").reset_index()
    by_journal = dict(tuple(per.groupby("journal_key")))           # one pass, not one filter per journal
    empty = per.iloc[0:0]
    rows = []
    for jk in sorted(arts["journal_key"].unique()):
        denom = int(with_org.get(jk, 0))
        mine = by_journal.get(jk, empty)
        reg = (registry_owner.get(jk) or "").strip()
        if reg:
            key = owner_key_of(reg)
            n = int(mine.loc[mine["org_key"] == key, "n"].sum()) if key else 0
            rows.append({"journal_key": jk, "owner_key": key, "owner_name": reg, "owner_source": "registry",
                         "owner_share": n / denom if denom else np.nan, "owner_matched": n > 0})
            continue
        if denom == 0 or mine.empty:
            rows.append({"journal_key": jk, "owner_key": "", "owner_name": "", "owner_source": None,
                         "owner_share": np.nan, "owner_matched": False})
            continue
        top = mine.sort_values(["n", "org_key"], ascending=[False, True]).iloc[0]
        share = int(top["n"]) / denom
        ok = share >= min_share
        rows.append({"journal_key": jk, "owner_key": top["org_key"] if ok else "",
                     "owner_name": display.get(top["org_key"], top["org_key"]) if ok else "",
                     "owner_source": "inferred" if ok else None, "owner_share": share if ok else np.nan,
                     "owner_matched": ok})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=OWNER_COLUMNS)


def author_metrics(arts: pd.DataFrame, authors: pd.DataFrame, owner_df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """In-house share, institutional diversity and foreign-author share per journal (articles of `arts`)."""
    orgs = _orgs(arts, authors)
    total = arts.groupby("journal_key")["article_id"].nunique()
    with_org = orgs.groupby("journal_key")["article_id"].nunique()
    foreign_ids: set = set()
    if len(authors):
        pairs = authors[["affiliation_th", "affiliation_en"]].fillna("").astype(str).drop_duplicates()
        pairs = pairs.assign(foreign=[is_foreign_affiliation(t, e) for t, e in
                                      zip(pairs["affiliation_th"], pairs["affiliation_en"])])
        fa = authors.merge(pairs, on=["affiliation_th", "affiliation_en"], how="left")
        foreign_ids = set(fa.loc[fa["foreign"].fillna(False).astype(bool), "article_id"])
    owner_of = owner_df.set_index("journal_key")["owner_key"].to_dict()
    by_journal = dict(tuple(orgs.groupby("journal_key")))
    empty = orgs.iloc[0:0]
    rows = []
    for jk, n_all in total.items():
        mine = by_journal.get(jk, empty)
        denom = int(with_org.get(jk, 0))
        counts = mine.groupby("org_key")["article_id"].nunique()
        p = counts / counts.sum() if counts.sum() else counts
        okey = owner_of.get(jk, "")
        inhouse = mine.loc[mine["org_key"] == okey, "article_id"].nunique() if okey else None
        arts_with_org = set(mine["article_id"])
        rows.append({
            "journal_key": jk, "with_org": denom, "affiliation_coverage": denom / int(n_all) if n_all else np.nan,
            "inhouse_share": inhouse / denom if (inhouse is not None and denom) else np.nan,
            "n_orgs": int(len(counts)), "effective_orgs": float(1.0 / (p ** 2).sum()) if len(p) else np.nan,
            "foreign_share": len(arts_with_org & foreign_ids) / denom if denom else np.nan,
        })
    return pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=AUTHOR_METRIC_COLUMNS)
```

### Task 3: Topic mix, peer journals (D4), scope drift (D1) and the special-issue finder (D5)

Pure functions over one row per assigned, dated article (`journal_key`, `topic_id`, `year`). Matrix work is dense journals × topics (2K × 2K floats ≈ 32 MB), drift is per journal over its own topics only.

**Files:**
- Create: `pipeline/atlas/journal_topics.py`, `pipeline/tests/test_journal_topics.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: scipy `jensenshannon`.
- Produces: `atlas.journal_topics` — `in_years(jt, start, end)`, `topic_mix(jt, start, end) -> DataFrame(journal_key, topic_id, count, share)` (sorted by journal, count desc), `peer_journals(mix, eligible, k, min_similarity) -> dict[key, list[(peer, cosine)]]`, `scope_drift(jt, years, ref_years, min_articles) -> DataFrame(journal_key, year, articles, distance)`, `topic_shifts(jt, before, after, n, min_articles) -> DataFrame(journal_key, topic_id, kind, share_before, share_after)`, `special_issue(jt_recent, status, statuses, min_host_articles, max_hosts) -> DataFrame(topic_id, hosts, top_host_share, articles_R)`, `journal_opportunities(opps, mix, edges, scope_topics, per_journal) -> DataFrame(journal_key, topic_id, reason, journal_articles, hosts)`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — create `pipeline/tests/test_journal_topics.py`:

```python
"""Topic mix, peer journals (D4), scope drift (D1) and the special-issue finder (D5)."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pytest

from atlas.journal_topics import (
    journal_opportunities,
    peer_journals,
    scope_drift,
    special_issue,
    topic_mix,
    topic_shifts,
)


def _jt(spec):
    """spec: {(journal, topic, year): n} → one row per article."""
    rows = [(j, t, y) for (j, t, y), n in spec.items() for _ in range(n)]
    return pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=["journal_key", "topic_id", "year"])


def test_topic_mix_shares_sum_to_one_per_journal():
    jt = _jt({("A", 1, 2020): 3, ("A", 2, 2021): 1, ("B", 2, 2021): 2, ("A", 1, 2010): 9})
    m = topic_mix(jt, 2020, 2021)
    a = m[m["journal_key"] == "A"].set_index("topic_id")
    assert a.loc[1, "count"] == 3 and a.loc[1, "share"] == pytest.approx(0.75)
    assert m.groupby("journal_key")["share"].sum().round(9).eq(1).all()


def test_peers_are_the_most_similar_eligible_journals():
    jt = _jt({("A", 1, 2022): 8, ("A", 2, 2022): 2, ("B", 1, 2022): 7, ("B", 2, 2022): 3,
              ("C", 3, 2022): 10, ("D", 1, 2022): 1})
    m = topic_mix(jt, 2022, 2022)
    peers = peer_journals(m, {"A", "B", "C"}, k=2, min_similarity=0.2)
    assert [p for p, _ in peers["A"]] == ["B"]                     # C shares no topic; D is not eligible
    assert peers["A"][0][1] == pytest.approx(0.62 / np.sqrt(0.68 * 0.58), abs=1e-4)
    assert peers["C"] == []
    assert peer_journals(m, {"A", "B"}, k=0, min_similarity=0.0) == {}


def test_scope_drift_is_zero_for_a_steady_journal_and_high_for_a_new_scope():
    steady = {("A", 1, y): 10 for y in range(2015, 2023)}
    moved = {("B", 1, y): 10 for y in range(2015, 2021)} | {("B", 2, 2021): 10, ("B", 2, 2022): 10}
    d = scope_drift(_jt(steady | moved), list(range(2018, 2023)), ref_years=3, min_articles=10)
    a = d[d["journal_key"] == "A"].set_index("year")["distance"]
    b = d[d["journal_key"] == "B"].set_index("year")["distance"]
    assert (a == 0).all() and b[2020] == 0 and b[2021] == pytest.approx(1.0)
    assert 0 < b[2022] < 1                                         # its reference years now mix both topics
    small = scope_drift(_jt({("C", 1, 2020): 3, ("C", 1, 2019): 30}), [2020], 3, 10)
    assert small["articles"].tolist() == [3] and np.isnan(small["distance"].iloc[0])


def test_topic_shifts_list_gains_and_losses():
    jt = _jt({("A", 1, 2016): 10, ("A", 2, 2016): 10, ("A", 1, 2023): 4, ("A", 3, 2023): 16})
    s = topic_shifts(jt, (2014, 2018), (2022, 2024), n=5, min_articles=10)
    gained = s[s["kind"] == "gained"].set_index("topic_id")
    lost = s[s["kind"] == "lost"].set_index("topic_id")
    assert list(gained.index) == [3] and gained.loc[3, "share_after"] == pytest.approx(0.8)
    assert list(lost.index) == [2, 1] and lost.loc[2, "share_before"] == pytest.approx(0.5)
    assert topic_shifts(jt, (2014, 2018), (2022, 2024), n=5, min_articles=50).empty


def test_special_issue_finds_emerging_topics_with_few_host_journals():
    jt = _jt({("A", 7, 2023): 5, ("B", 7, 2024): 1, ("A", 8, 2023): 4, ("B", 8, 2023): 4, ("C", 8, 2023): 4,
              ("A", 9, 2023): 9})
    status = pd.Series({7: "EMERGING", 8: "EMERGING", 9: "STABLE", 10: "WATCH"})
    s = special_issue(jt, status, ["EMERGING", "WATCH"], min_host_articles=3, max_hosts=2).set_index("topic_id")
    assert list(s.index) == [7]                                    # 8 has 3 hosts; 9 is STABLE; 10 has no articles
    assert s.loc[7, "hosts"] == 1 and s.loc[7, "top_host_share"] == pytest.approx(5 / 6)
    assert s.loc[7, "articles_R"] == 6


def test_opportunities_rank_in_scope_before_adjacent():
    opps = pd.DataFrame({"topic_id": [7, 11], "hosts": [1, 0], "top_host_share": [1.0, 0.0], "articles_R": [6, 2]})
    mix = topic_mix(_jt({("A", 7, 2023): 2, ("A", 1, 2023): 9, ("B", 1, 2023): 5}), 2023, 2023)
    edges = pd.DataFrame({"s": [1], "t": [11], "w": [0.7]})
    o = journal_opportunities(opps, mix, edges, scope_topics=5, per_journal=5)
    a = o[o["journal_key"] == "A"]
    assert a["topic_id"].tolist() == [7, 11] and a["reason"].tolist() == ["in_scope", "adjacent"]
    assert a["journal_articles"].tolist() == [2, 0]
    assert o[o["journal_key"] == "B"]["reason"].tolist() == ["adjacent"]
    assert journal_opportunities(opps, mix, edges.iloc[0:0], 5, 1)["topic_id"].tolist() == [7]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `pipeline/atlas/journal_topics.py`**

```python
"""Journal × topic analytics for editors: topic mix, peers (D4), scope drift (D1), special-issue finder (D5).

Input everywhere: `jt` = one row per assigned, dated article (journal_key, topic_id ≥ 0, year).

topic_mix        a journal's topics in [start, end]: count and share of its articles
peer_journals    cosine similarity of topic-mix vectors between eligible journals (≥ journals.min_articles)
scope_drift      per year: Jensen–Shannon distance (base 2, 0 = same mix, 1 = disjoint) between that year's topic
                 mix and the pooled mix of the drift_reference_years before it; null when either side has
                 fewer than drift_min_articles articles
topic_shifts     topics whose share of the journal grew / shrank most from window B to window R (emerging windows)
special_issue    topics in special_issue_statuses hosted by ≤ max_hosts journals, where a host published
                 ≥ min_host_articles of the topic's articles in R
journal_opportunities  per journal, those topics "in_scope" (it published some of them in the window) or
                 "adjacent" (an edge of the map joins them to one of its scope_topics largest topics)
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from scipy.spatial.distance import jensenshannon


def in_years(jt: pd.DataFrame, start: int, end: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
    return jt[(jt["year"] >= start) & (jt["year"] <= end)]


def topic_mix(jt: pd.DataFrame, start: int, end: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """journal_key, topic_id, count, share (of the journal's assigned articles in [start, end])."""
    w = in_years(jt, start, end)
    c = w.groupby(["journal_key", "topic_id"]).size().rename("count").reset_index()
    c["share"] = c["count"] / c.groupby("journal_key")["count"].transform("sum")
    return c.sort_values(["journal_key", "count", "topic_id"], ascending=[True, False, True]).reset_index(drop=True)


def peer_journals(mix: pd.DataFrame, eligible: set[str], k: int, min_similarity: float
                  ) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, float]]]:
    """{journal: [(peer, cosine)]} — the k most similar eligible journals with cosine ≥ min_similarity."""
    m = mix[mix["journal_key"].isin(eligible)]
    if m.empty or k == 0:
        return {}
    journals = sorted(m["journal_key"].unique())
    topics = sorted(m["topic_id"].unique())
    ji = {j: i for i, j in enumerate(journals)}
    ti = {t: i for i, t in enumerate(topics)}
    mat = np.zeros((len(journals), len(topics)))
    mat[m["journal_key"].map(ji).to_numpy(), m["topic_id"].map(ti).to_numpy()] = m["share"].to_numpy()
    norm = np.linalg.norm(mat, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    unit = mat / np.where(norm == 0, 1.0, norm)
    sim = unit @ unit.T
    np.fill_diagonal(sim, -1.0)
    out = {}
    for i, j in enumerate(journals):
        order = np.lexsort((np.arange(len(journals)), -sim[i]))     # highest first, ties by name order
        peers = [(journals[o], round(float(sim[i, o]), 4)) for o in order[:k] if sim[i, o] >= min_similarity]
        out[j] = peers
    return out


def scope_drift(jt: pd.DataFrame, years: list[int], ref_years: int, min_articles: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """journal_key, year, articles, distance (NaN when not computable) for every journal-year in `years`."""
    if not years:
        return pd.DataFrame(columns=["journal_key", "year", "articles", "distance"])
    lo, hi = min(years) - ref_years, max(years)
    rows = []
    for jk, g in in_years(jt, lo, hi).groupby("journal_key"):    # the reference years before `years` count too
        tab = g.groupby(["year", "topic_id"]).size().unstack(fill_value=0).reindex(range(lo, hi + 1), fill_value=0)
        for y in years:
            cur = tab.loc[y].to_numpy(dtype=float)
            ref = tab.loc[y - ref_years:y - 1].sum().to_numpy(dtype=float)
            n = int(cur.sum())
            if n == 0:
                continue
            dist = (float(jensenshannon(cur / cur.sum(), ref / ref.sum(), base=2))
                    if n >= min_articles and ref.sum() >= min_articles else np.nan)
            rows.append({"journal_key": jk, "year": int(y), "articles": n, "distance": dist})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=["journal_key", "year", "articles", "distance"])


def topic_shifts(jt: pd.DataFrame, before: tuple[int, int], after: tuple[int, int], n: int, min_articles: int
                 ) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """journal_key, topic_id, kind (gained | lost), share_before, share_after — the n largest share changes
    per journal, for journals with ≥ min_articles articles in both windows."""
    b, a = topic_mix(jt, *before), topic_mix(jt, *after)
    nb, na = b.groupby("journal_key")["count"].sum(), a.groupby("journal_key")["count"].sum()
    ok = set(nb[nb >= min_articles].index) & set(na[na >= min_articles].index)
    both = (b[b["journal_key"].isin(ok)][["journal_key", "topic_id", "share"]].rename(columns={"share": "share_before"})
            .merge(a[a["journal_key"].isin(ok)][["journal_key", "topic_id", "share"]]
                   .rename(columns={"share": "share_after"}), on=["journal_key", "topic_id"], how="outer")
            .fillna({"share_before": 0.0, "share_after": 0.0}))
    both["delta"] = both["share_after"] - both["share_before"]
    rows = []
    for _jk, g in both.groupby("journal_key"):
        up = g[g["delta"] > 0].sort_values(["delta", "topic_id"], ascending=[False, True]).head(n)
        down = g[g["delta"] < 0].sort_values(["delta", "topic_id"], ascending=[True, True]).head(n)
        rows += [up.assign(kind="gained"), down.assign(kind="lost")]
    cols = ["journal_key", "topic_id", "kind", "share_before", "share_after"]
    return pd.concat(rows, ignore_index=True)[cols] if rows else pd.DataFrame(columns=cols)


def special_issue(jt_recent: pd.DataFrame, status: pd.Series, statuses: list[str], min_host_articles: int,
                  max_hosts: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """topic_id, hosts, top_host_share, articles_R — topics of `statuses` with ≤ max_hosts host journals.
    jt_recent: the articles of window R; status: topic_id → status."""
    want = set(status[status.isin(statuses)].index)
    c = jt_recent[jt_recent["topic_id"].isin(want)].groupby(["topic_id", "journal_key"]).size().rename("n")
    present = set(c.index.get_level_values(0))
    rows = []
    for tid in sorted(want):
        per = c.loc[tid] if tid in present else pd.Series(dtype=int)
        total = int(per.sum())
        hosts = int((per >= min_host_articles).sum())
        if total == 0 or hosts > max_hosts:
            continue
        rows.append({"topic_id": int(tid), "hosts": hosts, "top_host_share": float(per.max() / total),
                     "articles_R": total})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=["topic_id", "hosts", "top_host_share", "articles_R"])


def journal_opportunities(opps: pd.DataFrame, mix: pd.DataFrame, edges: pd.DataFrame, scope_topics: int,
                          per_journal: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """journal_key, topic_id, reason (in_scope | adjacent), journal_articles, hosts — at most per_journal rows per
    journal: in_scope first (most of the journal's articles first), then adjacent; ties by fewer hosts, topic id."""
    cols = ["journal_key", "topic_id", "reason", "journal_articles", "hosts"]
    if opps.empty or mix.empty or per_journal == 0:
        return pd.DataFrame(columns=cols)
    hosts = opps.set_index("topic_id")["hosts"].to_dict()
    nbrs: dict[int, set[int]] = {}
    for s, t in zip(edges["s"].astype(int), edges["t"].astype(int)) if len(edges) else []:
        nbrs.setdefault(s, set()).add(t)
        nbrs.setdefault(t, set()).add(s)
    rows = []
    for jk, g in mix.groupby("journal_key"):
        have = dict(zip(g["topic_id"].astype(int), g["count"].astype(int)))
        top = g.sort_values(["count", "topic_id"], ascending=[False, True])["topic_id"].astype(int).head(scope_topics)
        near = set().union(*(nbrs.get(t, set()) for t in top)) if len(top) else set()
        found = []
        for tid in hosts:
            if tid in have:
                found.append((0, -have[tid], hosts[tid], tid, "in_scope", have[tid]))
            elif tid in near:
                found.append((1, 0, hosts[tid], tid, "adjacent", 0))
        for *_, tid, reason, n in sorted(found)[:per_journal]:
            rows.append({"journal_key": jk, "topic_id": int(tid), "reason": reason, "journal_articles": int(n),
                         "hosts": int(hosts[tid])})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows, columns=cols)
```

### Task 4: Research-methods lexicon, sample sizes and the `methods` step

A 16-method TH/EN regex lexicon and a sample-size reader, applied to title + keywords + abstracts on folded text; the step writes per-article rows (internal), topic summaries and corpus/yearly shares. Nothing textual is published.

**Files:**
- Create: `pipeline/atlas/methods.py`, `pipeline/tests/test_methods.py`
- Modify: `pipeline/atlas/config.py`, `pipeline/config.example.yaml`, `pipeline/atlas/pipeline.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `atlas.thai.clean_text`, `atlas.thai.fold_thai`; work `02_docs`, `04_fine_topics`, `06_meta.json`.
- Produces: `atlas.methods` — `Method`, `METHODS`, `METHOD_KEYS`, `LEXICON_VERSION` (8 hex), `fold`, `detect_methods(text) -> list[str]` (lexicon order), `sample_size(text, max_size) -> int | None`, `article_methods(docs, max_sample)`, `shares_of(methods: Series) -> (dict[method, n], n_with)`, `monoculture_share(methods, mono) -> float | None`, `in_window(year, start, end) -> bool Series`, `summarise_topics(am, start, end, mcfg)`, `by_year(am, years)`, `OUT_FILES`, step `methods(cfg, llm=None)` → `12_methods_articles` (article_id, year, topic_id, methods "a; b", sample_size Int64), `12_methods_topics` (topic_id, articles, with_methods, coverage, counts JSON, ss_n, ss_median, ss_q1, ss_q3, monoculture_share, monoculture), `12_methods_meta.json` {lexicon_version, window, articles, with_methods, counts, monoculture_share, by_year{years, articles, shares}}. `atlas.config.MethodsCfg` (`cfg["methods"]`).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — create `pipeline/tests/test_methods.py`:

```python
"""Research-method lexicon, sample sizes and the methods step (aggregates only)."""
import json

import pandas as pd
import pytest

from atlas.config import AtlasConfig, load_config
from atlas.io_utils import read_table, write_table
from atlas.methods import (
    LEXICON_VERSION,
    METHOD_KEYS,
    detect_methods,
    methods,
    monoculture_share,
    sample_size,
    summarise_topics,
)

MAI_EK = "\N{THAI CHARACTER MAI EK}"


def test_thai_and_english_methods_are_found_even_without_tone_marks():
    th = "การวิจัยนี้เก็บข้อมูลโดยใช้แบบสอบถาม วิเคราะห์ด้วยค่าเฉลี่ย และ t-test"
    assert detect_methods(th) == ["survey", "descriptive_stats", "inferential"]
    assert detect_methods("เป็นการวิจัยเชิงคุณภาพ ด้วยการสัมภาษณ์เชิงลึก") == ["interview", "qualitative"]
    damaged = "การวิจัยกึ่งทดลอง" + " " + "กลุ" + "มตัวอย" + "าง"            # tone marks lost in the PDF
    assert "experiment" in detect_methods(damaged)
    assert detect_methods("A quasi-experimental design with structural equation modelling (SEM)") == [
        "experiment", "sem"]
    assert detect_methods("Meaning-making in Thai poetry") == []           # "mean" only as a whole word
    assert len(LEXICON_VERSION) == 8 and "survey" in METHOD_KEYS


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text, n", [
    ("กลุ่มตัวอย่าง ได้แก่ นักเรียน จำนวน 400 คน", 400),
    ("กลุ" + "มตัวอย" + "าง 1,250 คน", 1250),
    ("ผู้ให้ข้อมูลสำคัญ 12 ราย", 12),
    ("data from 385 respondents were analysed", 385),
    ("a sample of 220 nurses", 220),
    ("n = 64", 64),
    ("the sample in 2019 was large", None),                   # a year is not a sample size
    ("กลุ่มตัวอย่างในปี 2562", None),                         # no unit → not read
    ("no numbers here", None),
])
def test_sample_size(text, n):
    assert sample_size(text) == n


def test_sample_size_takes_the_first_mention_and_respects_the_maximum():
    assert sample_size("n = 30 ... later 400 respondents") == 30
    assert sample_size("n = 5000000") is None
    assert sample_size("n = 5000000", max_size=10_000_000) == 5_000_000


def test_monoculture_share_counts_articles_using_only_the_listed_methods():
    m = pd.Series(["survey; descriptive_stats", "survey", "survey; inferential", "", "interview"])
    assert monoculture_share(m, ["survey", "descriptive_stats"]) == pytest.approx(2 / 4)
    assert monoculture_share(pd.Series(["", ""]), ["survey"]) is None


def test_topic_summary_skips_small_topics_and_reports_sample_sizes():
    cfg = load_config(None)["methods"] | {"min_articles": 3}
    am = pd.DataFrame({
        "article_id": list("abcdef"), "year": pd.array([2020, 2021, 2022, 2022, 2015, 2022], dtype="Int64"),
        "topic_id": [1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 2], "methods": ["survey; descriptive_stats", "survey", "interview", "", "survey",
                                                   "survey"],
        "sample_size": pd.array([400, 200, None, None, 50, 10], dtype="Int64")})
    s = summarise_topics(am, 2020, 2022, cfg)
    assert s["topic_id"].tolist() == [1]                     # topic 2 has 1 article; 2015 is outside the window
    row = s.iloc[0]
    assert row["articles"] == 4 and row["with_methods"] == 3 and row["coverage"] == pytest.approx(0.75)
    assert json.loads(row["counts"]) == {"survey": 2, "interview": 1, "descriptive_stats": 1}
    assert row["ss_n"] == 2 and row["ss_median"] == 300
    assert row["monoculture_share"] == pytest.approx(2 / 3) and bool(row["monoculture"]) is True


def test_unknown_monoculture_method_is_a_config_error():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="monoculture_methods"):
        AtlasConfig.model_validate({"methods": {"monoculture_methods": ["questionnaires"]}})


def test_methods_step_writes_aggregates_and_never_the_text(tmp_path):
    cfg = load_config(None)
    cfg["pipeline"].update(work_dir=str(tmp_path))
    cfg["methods"]["min_articles"] = 2
    docs = pd.DataFrame({"article_id": ["a", "b", "c"], "year": pd.array([2023, 2024, None], dtype="Int64"),
                         "title_th": ["", "", ""], "title_en": ["", "", ""],
                         "abstract_th": ["ใช้แบบสอบถามกับกลุ่มตัวอย่าง 300 คน", "การสัมภาษณ์เชิงลึก", "แบบสอบถาม"],
                         "abstract_en": ["", "", ""]})
    write_table(docs, tmp_path / "02_docs.parquet")
    write_table(pd.DataFrame({"article_id": ["a", "b", "c"], "fine_topic": [4, 4, -1]}),
                tmp_path / "04_fine_topics.parquet")
    (tmp_path / "06_meta.json").write_text(json.dumps({"last_complete_year": 2024, "years": [2023, 2024]}),
                                           encoding="utf-8")
    (tmp_path / "12_methods_notes.parquet").write_text("stale", encoding="utf-8")
    methods(cfg)
    arts = read_table(tmp_path / "12_methods_articles.parquet")
    assert set(arts.columns) == {"article_id", "year", "topic_id", "methods", "sample_size"}
    assert arts.set_index("article_id").loc["a", "sample_size"] == 300
    meta = json.loads((tmp_path / "12_methods_meta.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    assert meta["window"] == [2020, 2024] and meta["with_methods"] == 2
    assert meta["by_year"]["years"] == [2023, 2024] and meta["by_year"]["shares"]["survey"] == [1.0, 0.0]
    assert read_table(tmp_path / "12_methods_topics.parquet")["topic_id"].tolist() == [4]
    assert not (tmp_path / "12_methods_notes.parquet").exists()
    cfg["methods"]["enabled"] = False
    methods(cfg)
    assert not (tmp_path / "12_methods_topics.parquet").exists()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Config.** In `pipeline/atlas/config.py` insert the block below (followed by two blank lines) directly above `class ExportCfg(_Strict):`:

```python
class MethodsCfg(_Strict):
    """Research-method lexicon (TH/EN) + sample size, aggregated per topic, journal and year (P2)."""
    enabled: bool = True
    window_years: int = Field(5, ge=1)
    min_articles: int = Field(30, ge=1)   # topics with fewer window articles are not published in methods.json
    max_sample_size: int = Field(1_000_000, ge=2)
    monoculture_methods: list[str] = Field(default_factory=lambda: ["survey", "descriptive_stats"])
    monoculture_min_share: float = Field(0.6, gt=0.0, le=1.0)
    provider: Literal["llm", "off"] = "off"
    note_top_n: int = Field(50, ge=0)

    @field_validator("provider", mode="before")
    @classmethod
    def _provider_alias(cls, v):
        return "off" if v is False else v          # YAML reads a bare `off` as boolean false

    @field_validator("monoculture_methods")
    @classmethod
    def _known_methods(cls, v: list[str]) -> list[str]:
        from atlas.methods import METHOD_KEYS  # imported here: atlas.config stays free of analysis imports

        unknown = sorted(set(v) - set(METHOD_KEYS))
        if unknown or not v:
            raise ValueError(f"monoculture_methods must name methods from {list(METHOD_KEYS)} (unknown: {unknown})")
        return v
```
